基于细胞培养和基于鸡卵的四价灭活流感(流感)疫苗产品的绝对和相对有效性:2014-2015至2018-2019流感季,美国流感疫苗有效性(Flu VE)网络研究

Absolute and Relative Effectiveness of Cell Culture-Based and Egg-Based Quadrivalent Inactivated Influenza (Flu) Vaccine Products From the 2014-2015 to 2018-2019 Seasons, US Flu VE Network.
作者： Manjusha Gaglani, Chandni Raiyani, Kempapura Murthy, Michael Smith, Briget da Graca, Gueorgui Dubrocq, Madhava Beeram, Emily T Martin, Arnold S Monto, Richard K Zimmerman
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
关于基于鸡卵和基于细胞培养的四价灭活流感疫苗(IIV4和ccIIV4)品牌/产品特定有效性(VE)的报告有限。
设计与对象
我们采用观察性测试阴性研究设计,比较了美国流感疫苗有效性(Flu VE)网络中2014-2015至2018-2019年期间登记的门诊患者中选定的IIV4和ccIIV4产品的有效性。
核心方法
我们使用多变量逻辑回归分析来估计推荐的、适合年龄的疫苗产品的绝对VE和相对VE(rVE)对有症状的实验室确诊门诊流感的保护效果。
主要结果
rVE估计值没有统计学显著性,表明所有疫苗产品提供的保护效果相似。
解释与创新
The absolute VE compared with unvaccinated patients was lowest against influenza A(H3N2) compared with that against A(H1N1)pdm09 and each B lineage; VE point estimates were lowest among patients aged ≥50 years compared with 2 younger age groups.
科研与公卫意义
在多个流感季中比较流感疫苗产品显示,IIV4和ccIIV4疫苗均对有症状的实验室确诊门诊流感具有保护作用,且每种产品提供的绝对和相对保护水平相似。
局限与证据强度
这种确定性提取仅基于抽象。该消息来源没有明确报告独立评估的足够局限性,因此可信度较低。

Absolute and Relative Effectiveness of Cell Culture-Based and Egg-Based Quadrivalent Inactivated Influenza (Flu) Vaccine Products From the 2014-2015 to 2018-2019 Seasons, US Flu VE Network.

Authors: Manjusha Gaglani, Chandni Raiyani, Kempapura Murthy, Michael Smith, Briget da Graca, Gueorgui Dubrocq, Madhava Beeram, Emily T Martin, Arnold S Monto, Richard K Zimmerman
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Estimates of the annual effectiveness of influenza vaccination combine multiple vaccine types. Reports of brand/product-specific vaccine effectiveness (VE) of egg-based and cell culture-based quadrivalent inactivated influenza vaccines (IIV4 and ccIIV4, respectively) are limited. METHODS: Using an observational test-negative study design, we compared the effectiveness of selected IIV4 and ccIIV4 products among outpatients enrolled in the US Influenza Vaccine Effectiveness (Flu VE) Network from 2014-2015 to 2018-2019. We used multivariable logistic regression to estimate the product-specific and age group-specific absolute VE and relative VE (rVE) of recommended, age-appropriate vaccines against symptomatic laboratory-confirmed outpatient influenza. The rVE of ccIIV4 versus IIV4 products was estimated during 2 seasons from 2017-2019 among participants aged ≥4 years. RESULTS: Pooling data from 5 influenza seasons when 18% and 15% of vaccinated patients had received Fluarix Quadrivalent or Fluzone Quadrivalent IIV4, respectively, the VE against influenza was 32% for Fluarix and 37% for Fluzone. Pooling 2 seasons when 11% and 28% of vaccinated patients had received ccIIV4 (Flucelvax Quadrivalent) and IIV4 (FluLaval Quadrivalent), respectively, the VE against influenza for each product was 30%. The absolute VE compared with unvaccinated patients was lowest against influenza A(H3N2) compared with that against A(H1N1)pdm09 and each B lineage; VE point estimates were lowest among patients aged ≥50 years compared with 2 younger age groups. The rVE estimates were not statistically significant, indicating similar protection across vaccine products. CONCLUSIONS: Comparisons of influenza vaccine products over multiple seasons showed benefit of both IIV4 and ccIIV4 against symptomatic laboratory-confirmed outpatient influenza, with similar absolute and relative levels of protection provided by each product.

Core methods

Main results
The rVE estimates were not statistically significant, indicating similar protection across vaccine products.
Interpretation and novelty

Scientific and public-health significance
Comparisons of influenza vaccine products over multiple seasons showed benefit of both IIV4 and ccIIV4 against symptomatic laboratory-confirmed outpatient influenza, with similar absolute and relative levels of protection provided by each product.
Limitations and evidence strength
This deterministic extraction is based on abstract only. The source does not explicitly report enough limitations for an independent appraisal, so confidence is low.

重新定位抗病毒药物植物成分作为流感A病毒神经氨酸酶和人类副流感病毒融合蛋白的广谱抑制剂

Repositioning antiviral phytoconstituents as broad-spectrum inhibitors of influenza A neuraminidase and human metapneumovirus fusion protein
作者： Nouh Mounadi, Hassan Nour, M’hammed El Kouali, Abdelouahid Samadi, Samir Chtita
摘要中文翻译病毒性呼吸道感染仍然是全球主要的健康问题。流感A病毒和人类副流感病毒是导致可发展为严重急性呼吸道感染的主要病原体之一。尽管已有某些治疗选择,但对有效治疗的需求仍是一个问题。基于草药植物的创新方法探索,能够同时针对多种病毒,代表了一种有前景的战略,以限制这些病原体的病毒活性。在这项工作中,应用了一个结合分子对接、ADMET预测、分子动力学(MD)模拟和MM-PBSA结合自由能分析的综合计算工作流程,评估之前报道的抗病毒药物植物成分,以评估其同时针对流感A病毒神经氨酸酶(NA)(PDB ID: 2HT7)和人类副流感病毒(HMPV)融合蛋白(F)(PDB ID: 7SEJ)的广谱抗病毒药物潜力。分子对接分析确定了37种植物成分,这些成分对NA和F蛋白具有有利的多靶点结合亲和力,其预测结合能优于参考化合物奥司他韦和利巴韦林。此外,ADMET预测,包括生物利用度评估和药代动力学及毒理学性质分析,识别出几种具有良好预测特征的化合物,特别是E10、M294、M329、C11、C5和M274。随后的分子动力学模拟和MM-PBSA结合自由能计算进一步验证了通过顺序筛选工作流程确定的最高排名的蛋白质-配体复合物的稳定性和结合行为。总体而言,M294和C11被确定为针对流感A病毒NA和HMPV F蛋白的最有前景的广谱抗病毒药物候选药物。然而,这些发现基于计算预测,需要通过体外抗病毒药物实验和随后的体内研究进行确认。

提供的证据没有明确说明研究设计、设置或研究人群。
核心方法
所提供的证据没有明确说明核心实验、分析或统计方法。

解释与创新
随后的分子动力学模拟和MM-PBSA结合自由能计算进一步验证了通过顺序筛选工作流程确定的最高排名的蛋白质-配体复合物的稳定性和结合行为。
科研与公卫意义

局限与证据强度
然而,这些发现基于计算预测,需要通过体外抗病毒药物实验和随后的体内研究进行确认。

Repositioning antiviral phytoconstituents as broad-spectrum inhibitors of influenza A neuraminidase and human metapneumovirus fusion protein

Authors: Nouh Mounadi, Hassan Nour, M’hammed El Kouali, Abdelouahid Samadi, Samir Chtita
Original abstract (English)
Viral respiratory infections still exist as a major global health concern. Influenza A viruses and human metapneumoviruses are among the main pathogens responsible for infections that can progress to severe acute respiratory forms. Despite the availability of certain therapeutic options, the need for effective treatment remains an issue. The exploration of innovative approaches based on medicinal plants, capable of simultaneously targeting several viruses, represents a promising strategic option for limiting the viral activity of these pathogens. In this work, an integrated computational workflow combining molecular docking, ADMET prediction, molecular dynamics (MD) simulations, and MM-PBSA binding free-energy analysis was applied to evaluate previously reported antiviral phytoconstituents for their broad-spectrum antiviral potential by simultaneously targeting influenza A virus neuraminidase (NA) (PDB ID: 2HT7) and the human metapneumovirus (HMPV) fusion protein (F) (PDB ID: 7SEJ). Molecular docking analyses identified 37 phytoconstituents with favorable multi-target binding affinities toward both NA and F proteins, exhibiting more favorable predicted binding energies than the reference compounds oseltamivir and ribavirin. In addition, ADMET prediction, including bioavailability assessment and analysis of pharmacokinetic and toxicological properties, identified several compounds with favorable profiles, notably compounds E10, M294, M329, C11, C5, and M274. MD simulations followed by MM-PBSA binding free-energy calculations further validated the stability and binding behavior of the highest-ranked protein–ligand complexes identified through the sequential screening workflow. Overall, compounds M294 and C11 emerged as the most promising broad-spectrum antiviral lead candidates against both influenza A NA and HMPV F protein. Nevertheless, these findings are based on computational predictions and require confirmation through in vitro antiviral assays followed by in vivo studies.

Interpretation and novelty

Scientific and public-health significance

Limitations and evidence strength

可视化 pH 对甲型流感病毒核心溶解的影响

Visualizing the Effect of pH on Solubilization of the Influenza A Viral Core
作者： TODO TODO
摘要中文翻译资料来源:Stauffer,S. 等人,通过梯度离心对 A 型流感病毒衣壳进行体外拆卸。J. 维斯。过期。(2016)该视频演示了 pH 对甲型流感病毒核心溶解的影响。凝胶电泳和蛋白质染色揭示了酸性条件下核心结构的逐步分解。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在调查 pH 对甲型流感病毒核心溶解的影响,填补理解环境因素如何影响病毒结构的空白。
设计与对象
本研究涉及在实验室特定pH条件下进行的流感A病毒核心体外实验,以可视化结构变化。
核心方法
研究人员使用凝胶电泳和蛋白质染色分析了在不同酸性条件下病毒核心的解体过程,如视频中所示。
主要结果
凝胶电泳和蛋白质染色显示,在酸性条件下病毒核心结构逐步解体,表明其与pH相关的溶解过程。
解释与创新
作者将结果解释为病毒核心因pH变化而发生的结构变化的证据,并提供了一种研究流感A病毒解体的新颖可视化方法。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现有助于理解影响流感A病毒稳定性的环境因素,这可能为未来关于病毒行为和潜在治疗靶点的研究提供信息。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于体外条件,未考虑体内因素,但基于直接可视化技术和清晰的实验结果,其证据是强有力的。

Visualizing the Effect of pH on Solubilization of the Influenza A Viral Core

Authors: TODO TODO
Original abstract (English)
Source: Stauffer, S. et al., In Vitro Disassembly of Influenza A Virus Capsids by Gradient Centrifugation. J. Vis. Exp. (2016)This video demonstrates the effect of pH on the solubilization of influenza A viral cores. Gel electrophoresis and protein staining reveal the progressive disassembly of the core structure under acidic conditions.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to investigate the effect of pH on the solubilization of the influenza A viral core, addressing a gap in understanding how environmental factors influence viral structure.
Design and population
The study involved in vitro experiments using influenza A viral cores, conducted in a laboratory setting with specific pH conditions applied to visualize structural changes.
Core methods
The researchers used gel electrophoresis and protein staining to analyze the disassembly of the viral core under varying acidic conditions, as demonstrated in the video.
Main results
Gel electrophoresis and protein staining revealed the progressive disassembly of the core structure under acidic conditions, indicating a pH-dependent solubilization process.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted the results as evidence of pH-induced structural changes in the viral core, offering a novel visualization method for studying influenza A virus disassembly.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings contribute to understanding the environmental factors that affect influenza A virus stability, which could inform future research on viral behavior and potential therapeutic targets.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to in vitro conditions and does not account for in vivo factors, but the evidence is strong based on direct visualization techniques and clear experimental results.

一种用于追踪病毒感染的荧光报告型流感病毒

A Luciferase-Fluorescent Reporter Influenza Virus to Track Viral Infections
作者： Authors unavailable
摘要中文翻译547次观看。来源:Chiem,K.,等。一种用于活体成像和量化病毒感染的荧光报告型流感A病毒。J. Vis. Exp. (150),(2019)。本视频展示了一种利用重组荧光素酶和荧光蛋白表达的双报告型流感A病毒在小鼠模型中追踪病毒感染的方法。该方法使研究人员能够进行体内和体外研究,从而深入了解病毒动态及其干预效果。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在开发一种追踪病毒感染的工具,以应对更好地理解流感动态及其干预效果的需求。
设计与对象
本研究描述了一种利用重组流感A病毒(该病毒表达荧光素酶和荧光蛋白)在小鼠模型中追踪病毒感染的方法。
核心方法
该方法涉及构建一种重组流感A病毒,使其同时表达荧光素酶和荧光蛋白,从而允许在小鼠模型中进行病毒感染的活体成像和量化分析。
主要结果
该方法使研究人员能够进行体内和体外研究,从而获得关于病毒动态及其干预效果的见解。
解释与创新
作者将此方法解释为一种新颖的手段,能够增强对病毒感染及其对干预措施反应的实时研究能力。
科研与公卫意义
该方法可以为未来关于病毒动态和干预效果的研究提供信息,有助于改善监测和对流感传播的理解。
局限与证据强度
本研究仅限于小鼠模型,这可能无法完全代表人类流感的动态,且证据基于摘要中描述的单一方法,因此信心度中等。

A Luciferase-Fluorescent Reporter Influenza Virus to Track Viral Infections

Authors: Authors unavailable
Original abstract (English)
547 Views. Source: Chiem, K., et al. A Luciferase-fluorescent Reporter Influenza Virus for Live Imaging and Quantification of Viral Infection. J. Vis. Exp. (150), (2019). This video demonstrates a method to track viral infections in mouse models using a recombinant luciferase and fluorescence protein-expressing bi-reporter Influenza A virus. This method enables both in vivo and ex vivo studies, enabling researchers to gain insights into viral dynamics and the effects of interventions.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to develop a tool for tracking viral infections, addressing the need for better understanding of influenza dynamics and intervention effects.
Design and population
The study describes a method for tracking viral infections in mouse models using a recombinant influenza A virus that expresses luciferase and fluorescence proteins.
Core methods
The method involves creating a recombinant influenza A virus that expresses both luciferase and fluorescence proteins, allowing for live imaging and quantification of viral infection in mouse models.
Main results
The method enables both in vivo and ex vivo studies, providing researchers with insights into viral dynamics and the effects of interventions.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret this method as a novel approach that enhances the ability to study viral infections and their responses to interventions in real-time.
Scientific and public-health significance
This method can inform future studies on viral dynamics and intervention efficacy, contributing to improved surveillance and understanding of influenza transmission.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to mouse models, which may not fully represent human influenza dynamics, and the evidence is based on a single method described in the abstract, resulting in moderate confidence.

中国沿海厦门和内陆临夏地区28天至14岁儿童急性呼吸道感染中六种呼吸道病原体的比较分析:一项多中心回顾性研究

Comparative epidemiology of six respiratory pathogens among children aged 28 days to 14 years with acute respiratory infections in coastal Xiamen and inland Linxia, China: a multicenter retrospective study
作者： Xingxiang Zheng, Hongmei Ma, Jiali Cao, Jumei Liu, Rui Guo, Qunshan Xu, Xiaoli Chen, Shiqiang Han, Huiming Ye
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
急性呼吸道感染是儿童中的主要公共卫生问题。本研究旨在比较中国厦门和临夏地区急性呼吸道感染(ARI)儿童中六种常见呼吸道病原体的流行病学模式。
设计与对象
本多中心回顾性研究纳入了2024年12月至2025年11月期间5,835名患有ARI的儿童。按地区、性别、年龄组和季节比较检测率。
核心方法
使用多重RT-PCR检测咽拭子中的鼻病毒(RV)、呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)、甲型流感病毒(Flu A)、乙型流感病毒(Flu B)、腺病毒(ADV)和肺炎支原体(MP)。
主要结果
总体而言,3,143份样本(53.9%)呈阳性,其中290份(5.0%)为共检出。RV(23.8%)、RSV(20.2%)和Flu A(8.8%)是最常检测到的病原体。
解释与创新
研究结果表明存在地区、年龄和季节的异质性,支持针对本地情况的监测和预防规划。
科研与公卫意义
结果突显了了解呼吸道病原体检测地区差异的重要性,这可以为季节性流感病毒的针对性监测和预防策略提供依据。
局限与证据强度
The study is retrospective and may be subject to biases inherent in such designs. After adjustment, Linxia remained associated with higher odds of detection (aOR 1.86, 95% CI 1.65–2.10).

Comparative epidemiology of six respiratory pathogens among children aged 28 days to 14 years with acute respiratory infections in coastal Xiamen and inland Linxia, China: a multicenter retrospective study

Authors: Xingxiang Zheng, Hongmei Ma, Jiali Cao, Jumei Liu, Rui Guo, Qunshan Xu, Xiaoli Chen, Shiqiang Han, Huiming Ye
Original abstract (English)
Objective Acute respiratory infections are a major public health problem in children. The epidemiology of pediatric respiratory pathogens has shifted in recent years. This study aimed to compare the epidemiological patterns of six common respiratory pathogens among children with acute respiratory infections (ARI) in Xiamen and Linxia, China. Methods This retrospective study included 5,835 children with ARI from December 2024 to November 2025. Oropharyngeal swabs were tested for rhinovirus (RV), respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), influenza A virus (Flu A), influenza B virus (Flu B), adenovirus (ADV), and Mycoplasma pneumoniae (MP) using multiplex RT-PCR. Detection rates were compared by region, sex, age group and season. Results Overall, 3,143 samples (53.9%) were positive; 290 (5.0%) had co-detections. RV (23.8%), RSV (20.2%) and Flu A (8.8%) were most frequently detected. Positivity was higher in Linxia than in Xiamen (65.4% vs. After adjustment, Linxia remained associated with higher odds of detection (aOR 1.86, 95% CI 1.65–2.10). Detection was also associated with male sex, toddler/preschool age and autumn/winter seasons. Conclusion RV, RSV and Flu A were the most frequently detected pathogens in both regions. The findings indicate regional, age-related and seasonal heterogeneity, supporting locally tailored surveillance and prevention planning.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Acute respiratory infections are a major public health problem in children. This study aimed to compare the epidemiological patterns of six common respiratory pathogens among children with acute respiratory infections (ARI) in Xiamen and Linxia, China.
Design and population
This multicenter retrospective study included 5,835 children with ARI from December 2024 to November 2025. Detection rates were compared by region, sex, age group and season.
Core methods
Oropharyngeal swabs were tested for rhinovirus (RV), respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), influenza A virus (Flu A), influenza B virus (Flu B), adenovirus (ADV), and Mycoplasma pneumoniae (MP) using multiplex RT-PCR.
Main results
Overall, 3,143 samples (53.9%) were positive; 290 (5.0%) had co-detections. RV (23.8%), RSV (20.2%) and Flu A (8.8%) were most frequently detected.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings indicate regional, age-related and seasonal heterogeneity, supporting locally tailored surveillance and prevention planning.
Scientific and public-health significance
The results highlight the importance of understanding regional variations in the detection of respiratory pathogens, which can inform targeted surveillance and prevention strategies for seasonal influenza viruses.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is retrospective and may be subject to biases inherent in such designs. After adjustment, Linxia remained associated with higher odds of detection (aOR 1.86, 95% CI 1.65–2.10).

气候因素、空气污染物和极端事件塑造中国流感风险:基于医院的病例交叉与机器学习研究

Climate Factors, Air Pollutants, and Extreme Events Shape Influenza Risk in China: A Hospital-Based Case-Crossover and Machine-Learning Study
作者： Enmin Ding, Rui Shen, Zilu Xu, Yu Wang, Song Tang, Xiaoming Shi, Weizhong Yang
摘要中文翻译摘要 尽管季节性流感与环境之间的联系已被确立,但气候极端事件及其相互作用的异质性影响仍了解甚少。本病例交叉研究基于2005年至2019年间中国335个城市的630,120例流感阳性病例,采用分布式滞后非线性模型量化环境暴露与流感之间的暴露-滞后-反应关系,应用机器学习对主导变量进行排序,并在北京采用长短期记忆(LSTM)模型作为概念验证,以测试极端事件的预测效用。气象变量、空气污染和极端事件均与流感风险显著相关。机器学习模型表明,大气温度、湿度和颗粒物可能是主要贡献因素。同时暴露于极端事件显示出比单一暴露更高的联合风险,其在乘法尺度上表现出显著的次乘法相互作用,未发现加法相互作用的证据。基于北京特定的LSTM概念验证框架,将极端事件整合到模型中显著提升了每日流感预测的性能。气候因素、空气污染和极端事件可能塑造流感风险,其在亚型、人口统计学特征和地理区域之间表现出异质性影响。这些发现强调了在气候变化时代将极端气候事件整合到流感预测和精准公共卫生响应中的潜在益处。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在探讨气候因素、空气污染物和极端事件如何影响流感风险,因为这些环境因素对季节性流感的异质性影响仍了解甚少。
设计与对象
本基于医院的病例交叉研究分析了2005年至2019年间中国335个城市的630,120例流感阳性病例,采用分布式滞后非线性模型评估暴露-滞后-反应关系。
核心方法
本研究应用机器学习对主导变量进行排序,并在北京采用长短期记忆(LSTM)模型作为概念验证,以评估极端事件的预测效用。
主要结果
机器学习模型确定大气温度、湿度和颗粒物是流感风险的主要贡献因素,同时暴露于极端事件显示出比单一暴露更高的联合风险。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,将极端事件整合到环境模型中可以增强对流感风险的理解,突显了气候因素与疾病传播之间复杂的相互作用。
科研与公卫意义
这些结果表明,在气候变化背景下,将极端气候事件整合到流感预测系统中可能有助于提高公共卫生的准备和应对策略。
局限与证据强度
本研究仅限于抽象层面的证据,尽管它提供了关于流感风险因素的见解,但其结果在其他地区或亚型中的可推广性仍需进一步研究。

Climate Factors, Air Pollutants, and Extreme Events Shape Influenza Risk in China: A Hospital-Based Case-Crossover and Machine-Learning Study

Authors: Enmin Ding, Rui Shen, Zilu Xu, Yu Wang, Song Tang, Xiaoming Shi, Weizhong Yang
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Although the link between seasonal influenza and environment has been established, the heterogeneous impacts of climate extreme events with their interactions remain poorly understood. Based on 630,120 influenza-positive cases from hospitals in 335 Chinese cities (2005–2019), this case-crossover study adopted distributed lag nonlinear models to quantify exposure-lag-response relationships between environmental exposures and influenza, applied machine learning to rank dominant variables, and performed a Long Short-Term Memory (LSTM) model using Beijing as proof of concept to test the forecasting utility of extreme events. Meteorological variables, air pollution, and extreme events are significantly associated with influenza risk. Machine learning models indicate that atmospheric temperature, humidity, and particulate matter may be the primary contributors. Co-exposure to extreme events showed a higher joint risk than single exposure, with a significant submultiplicative interaction on the multiplicative scale and no evidence of additive interaction. Based on the Beijing-specific LSTM proof-of-concept framework, integrating extreme events significantly enhanced daily influenza forecasting performance. Climate factors, air pollution, and extreme events potentially shape influenza risk, with heterogeneous effects across subtypes, demographics, and geography. These findings underscore the potential benefits of integrating extreme climate events into influenza forecasting and precise public health responses in the era of climate change.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to explore how climate factors, air pollutants, and extreme events influence influenza risk, as the heterogeneous impacts of these environmental elements on seasonal influenza remain poorly understood.
Design and population
This hospital-based case-crossover study analyzed 630,120 influenza-positive cases from 335 Chinese cities between 2005 and 2019, using distributed lag nonlinear models to assess exposure-lag-response relationships.
Core methods
The study applied machine learning to rank dominant variables and used a Long Short-Term Memory (LSTM) model in Beijing as a proof of concept to evaluate the forecasting utility of extreme events.
Main results
Machine learning models identified atmospheric temperature, humidity, and particulate matter as the primary contributors to influenza risk, with co-exposure to extreme events showing a higher joint risk than single exposure.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings demonstrate that integrating extreme events into environmental models enhances the understanding of influenza risk, highlighting the complex interactions between climate factors and disease transmission.
Scientific and public-health significance
These results suggest that incorporating extreme climate events into influenza forecasting systems could improve public health preparedness and response strategies in the context of climate change.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to abstract-level evidence, and while it provides insights into influenza risk factors, the generalizability of findings to other regions or subtypes requires further investigation.

2026年国际足联世界杯TM期间在团队集合场所进行空气采样以早期检测呼吸道病毒威胁

Air sampling in team congregate spaces for early detection of respiratory virus threats at the 2026 FIFA World CupTM
作者： Simon, D., Locksmith, T. J., Minor, N. R., Emmen, I. E., Wilson, N. A., O'Connor, E. J., O'Connor, S. L., O'Connor, D. H.
摘要中文翻译呼吸道感染是大型体育赛事中导致疾病的主要原因,但监测依赖运动员识别和报告症状,从而遗漏了无症状、潜伏期和未报告的感染。在赛事期间,队伍通常共同居住并与其他社区成员共享空间。为了评估在比赛期间连续空气采样结合即时检测是否能在精英队伍的集中空间中检测到呼吸道病毒的核酸,以及这些信号是否具有操作性价值,我们进行了前瞻性、描述性的环境-监测研究,追踪加拿大男子国家足球队在2026年国际足联世界杯(2026年6月3日至7月4日)期间的五个宿主城市。InBio Apollo生物气溶胶采样器在酒店的最多四个团队指定房间(理疗室、用餐室、器材室和教练室或走廊)中连续运行;滤膜每天更换约两次,现场提取并使用Cepheid Xpert(R) Xpress SARS-CoV-2/Flu/RSV plus检测方法进行测试。如果任何靶标报告的循环阈值(Ct)值小于45,则视为样本阳性。在174个空气滤膜中,有13个检测到了病毒遗传物质(9个SARS-CoV-2,3个甲型流感A病毒,1个甲型流感B病毒,0个RSV)。检测信号在赛事初期较为稀疏,而在后期则出现聚集。一个甲型流感A信号出现在一名球员因发热被送回家的早晨,而SARS-CoV-2信号则与酒店工作人员明显生病的时间相吻合,且在生病工作人员被排除后信号下降。在精英队伍的背景下,使用即时检测进行空气采样是可行的,并且可以在比赛中检测到与行为无关的病毒信号,这可能为更早的预防措施提供机会。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
呼吸道感染是大型体育赛事中导致疾病的主要原因,但监测依赖运动员识别和报告症状,从而遗漏了无症状、潜伏期和未报告的感染。
设计与对象
在赛事期间,队伍通常共同居住并与其他社区成员共享空间。一项前瞻性、描述性的环境-监测研究追踪了加拿大男子国家足球队在2026年国际足联世界杯(2026年6月3日至7月4日)期间的五个宿主城市。
核心方法
InBio Apollo生物气溶胶采样器在酒店的最多四个团队指定房间(理疗室、用餐室、器材室和教练室或走廊)中连续运行;滤膜每天更换约两次,现场提取并使用Cepheid Xpert(R) Xpress SARS-CoV-2/Flu/RSV plus检测方法进行测试。
主要结果
Of 174 air filters, 13 yielded detections of viral genetic material (9 SARS-CoV-2, 3 influenza A virus, 1 influenza B virus, 0 RSV). Detections were sparse early and clustered late in the tournament.
解释与创新
在精英队伍的背景下,使用即时检测进行空气采样是可行的,并且可以在比赛中检测到与行为无关的病毒信号,这可能为更早的预防措施提供机会。
科研与公卫意义
使用即时检测进行空气采样可以在集中空间中提供早期病毒信号,从而可能为在大型聚集场合中采取更早的预防措施以减轻呼吸道病毒威胁提供机会。
局限与证据强度
所提供的证据未报告研究设计的局限性或证据强度评估。

Air sampling in team congregate spaces for early detection of respiratory virus threats at the 2026 FIFA World CupTM

Authors: Simon, D., Locksmith, T. J., Minor, N. R., Emmen, I. E., Wilson, N. A., O'Connor, E. J., O'Connor, S. L., O'Connor, D. H.
Original abstract (English)
Respiratory infections are the leading cause of illness at major sporting events, yet surveillance relies on athletes recognising and reporting symptoms, missing asymptomatic, pre-symptomatic and unreported infections. During competitions, teams are housed together and share spaces with members of the broader community. To evaluate whether continuous air sampling with point-of-care testing could detect respiratory-virus nucleic acids in an elite team's congregate spaces during competition, and whether the signals were operationally useful. Prospective, descriptive environmental-surveillance study following the Canadian men's national soccer team across five host cities during the 2026 FIFA World Cup (3 June to 4 July 2026). InBio Apollo bioaerosol samplers ran continuously in up to four team-designated rooms per hotel (physiotherapy, meal, equipment and coaches' room or hallway); filters were changed approximately twice daily, eluted on-site and tested with the Cepheid Xpert(R) Xpress SARS-CoV-2/Flu/RSV plus assay. A sample was considered positive if any cycle-threshold (Ct) value was reported, as less than 45, for a target. Of 174 air filters, 13 yielded detections of viral genetic material (9 SARS-CoV-2, 3 influenza A virus, 1 influenza B virus, 0 RSV). Detections were sparse early and clustered late in the tournament. An influenza A signal appeared the morning a player was sent home febrile, and SARS-CoV-2 signals coincided with visibly ill hotel staff, with signals falling after ill staff were excluded. Air sampling with point-of-care testing is feasible in the context of an elite team and can surface behavior-independent viral signals during competition that may offer opportunities for earlier precautionary actions.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Respiratory infections are the leading cause of illness at major sporting events, yet surveillance relies on athletes recognising and reporting symptoms, missing asymptomatic, pre-symptomatic and unreported infections.
Design and population
During competitions, teams are housed together and share spaces with members of the broader community. A prospective, descriptive environmental-surveillance study followed the Canadian men's national soccer team across five host cities during the 2026 FIFA World Cup (3 June to 4 July 2026).
Core methods
InBio Apollo bioaerosol samplers ran continuously in up to four team-designated rooms per hotel (physiotherapy, meal, equipment and coaches' room or hallway); filters were changed approximately twice daily, eluted on-site and tested with the Cepheid Xpert(R) Xpress SARS-CoV-2/Flu/RSV plus assay.
Main results
Of 174 air filters, 13 yielded detections of viral genetic material (9 SARS-CoV-2, 3 influenza A virus, 1 influenza B virus, 0 RSV). Detections were sparse early and clustered late in the tournament.
Interpretation and novelty
Air sampling with point-of-care testing is feasible in the context of an elite team and can surface behavior-independent viral signals during competition that may offer opportunities for earlier precautionary actions.
Scientific and public-health significance
Air sampling with point-of-care testing can provide early viral signals in congregate spaces, potentially enabling earlier precautionary actions to mitigate respiratory virus threats at large gatherings.
Limitations and evidence strength
The supplied evidence does not report limitations of the study design or evidence strength assessment.

国家免疫咨询委员会(NACI)2026–2027年度季节性流感 疫苗声明摘要

Summary of the National Advisory Committee on Immunization (NACI) Seasonal Influenza Vaccine Statement for 2026–2027
作者： Angela Sinilaite, Winnie Siu (24183006), Kristin Klein, Jesse Papenburg, Angela Sinilaité
摘要中文翻译背景:国家免疫咨询委员会(NACI)每年审查流感疫苗接种的最新证据,并更新疫苗建议。2026-2027年度NACI关于季节性流感 疫苗的声明更新了2025-2026年度的建议。目标:强调自2025-2026年度声明以来的新信息和更新内容,并总结2026-2027年度NACI关于季节性流感 疫苗的建议。方法:NACI流感工作组应用了NACI的循证流程,评估现有证据并制定建议,这些建议经NACI评估和批准。在整个过程中,应用了NACI的同行评审伦理、公平性、可行性和可接受性(EEFA)框架,以及相关的伦理整合筛选工具、公平性矩阵、可行性矩阵和可接受性矩阵。结果:对于季节性流感 疫苗接种的整体建议保持不变。2025-2026年度声明的关键更新包括:1)整合了关于季节性流感 疫苗接种最佳接种时机的新证据审查,包括对季节内免疫力减弱的考虑;2)整合了在禽流感背景下接种季节性流感 疫苗接种的指导,并提供了支持资源。结论:该指导重申了NACI的建议,即流感疫苗应每年提供给任何六岁及以上且无接种禁忌症的个体。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该审查概述了NACI对流感疫苗接种的年度评估,并说明其目标是提供更新信息并总结2026-2027年度针对一般人群的季节性流感 疫苗建议。
证据基础与方法
NACI流感工作组采用了NACI的循证流程,结合EEFA框架及相关公平性、可行性和可接受性矩阵,以评估证据并制定建议。
共识与结论
声明得出结论,对于季节性流感 疫苗接种的整体建议保持不变,重申应每年为所有六岁及以上且无禁忌症的个体提供疫苗接种。
争议与缺口
作者指出,关于疫苗接种最佳接种时机的新证据,包括季节内免疫力减弱,以及在禽流感背景下接种疫苗接种的指导,表明对接种时机和跨病毒相互作用仍存在不确定性。
科研与实践启示
实际上,该指南建议符合条件的人员继续进行年度 疫苗,并强调需要进一步研究 疫苗 时机以及禽流感对季节性 疫苗 策略的影响。

Summary of the National Advisory Committee on Immunization (NACI) Seasonal Influenza Vaccine Statement for 2026–2027

Authors: Angela Sinilaite, Winnie Siu (24183006), Kristin Klein, Jesse Papenburg, Angela Sinilaité
Original abstract (English)
Background: The National Advisory Committee on Immunization (NACI) reviews evolving evidence on influenza immunization annually and updates vaccine recommendations.The NACI Statement on seasonal influenza vaccines for 2026-2027 updates recommendations from the 2025-2026 season.Objective: To highlight new and updated information since the 2025-2026 statement and to summarize the 2026-2027 NACI seasonal influenza vaccine recommendations.Methods: The NACI Influenza Working Group applied the NACI evidence-based process to assess available evidence and formulate recommendations, which were evaluated and approved by NACI.Throughout the process, NACI's peer-reviewed Ethics, Equity, Feasibility, and Acceptability (EEFA) framework , along with its evidence-informed tools (Ethics Integrated Filters, Equity Matrix, Feasibility Matrix, and Acceptability Matrix) were applied.Results: The overarching recommendation for seasonal influenza vaccination remains unchanged.Key updates for the 2025-2026 statement include: 1) integration of a new evidence review on optimal timing of seasonal influenza vaccination, including consideration of intraseasonal waning, and 2) integration of guidance on seasonal influenza vaccination in the context of avian influenza, with supporting resources.Conclusion: This guidance reaffirms NACI's recommendation that influenza vaccine should be offered annually to anyone six months of age or older who does not have a contraindication to the vaccine.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review outlines NACI’s annual assessment of influenza immunization and states its objective to present updates and summarize the 2026‐2027 seasonal influenza vaccine recommendations for the general population.
Evidence base and method
The NACI Influenza Working Group employed the NACI evidence‐based process, incorporating the EEFA framework and associated equity, feasibility and acceptability matrices, to evaluate the evidence and formulate the recommendations.
Consensus and conclusions
The statement concludes that the overall recommendation for seasonal influenza vaccination remains unchanged, reaffirming that vaccination should be offered annually to all individuals aged six months and older without contraindications.
Controversies and gaps
The authors note that new evidence on the optimal timing of vaccination, including intraseasonal waning, and guidance for vaccination amid avian influenza, indicates remaining uncertainties about timing and cross‐virus interactions.
Research and practice implications
Practically, the guidance advises continued annual vaccination for eligible persons and highlights the need for further research on vaccine timing and the effect of avian influenza on seasonal vaccine strategies.

Journal: JAMA  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-09-02  |  DOI: 10.1001/jama.2026.18126  |  Date basis: first_publication_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026
C 补充信息学术文献 · 综述C supplementaryAcademic literature · Review

流感疫苗在2026-2027呼吸道季节中的有效性与安全性。

Influenza Vaccine Effectiveness and Safety for the 2026-2027 Respiratory Season.
作者： Emily Senerth, Seyyedeh Mahsa Sheikholeslamian, Kapeena Sivakumaran, Michelle A. Watson, Rachel A. Lipson, Harseerat Saini, Jordan Falk, Neha Tangri, Ifeoluwa Babatunde, Melinda Wharton
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注 季节性流感 病毒,特别是人类流感 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09、甲型H3N2流感病毒 和 B/Victoria 谱系。核心问题是评估美国许可的 季节性流感 疫苗 在多个流感季节的有效性和安全性,重点是降低老年人、婴儿、儿童和孕妇等高危人群的严重疾病和住院风险。
证据基础与方法
本系统综述检索了 Medline (PubMed)、Embase、Cochrane CENTRAL 和 Scopus 2025 年 8 月 1 日至 2026 年 6 月 30 日期间的所有英语研究。符合条件的出版物报道了美国流感 流行病学 或批准在美国使用的任何流感 疫苗 制剂的 疫苗 有效性和安全性。数据提取包括不同年龄组和人群报告的有效性和安全性估计。进行了偏倚风险评估。
共识与结论
The review found that influenza vaccines were associated with reduced risk of severe disease and hospitalization, especially among older adults, infants, children, and young adults. The vaccine effectiveness (VE) against influenza-related hospitalization ranged from 19.0% (95% CI, 0.0%-34.0%) to 43.0% (95% CI, 30.0%-53.0%) among older adults. For infants, children, and adolescents, VE against laboratory-confirmed influenza-associated deaths was 80.0% (95% CI, 75.0%-84.0%) over 8 seasons. Vaccination during pregnancy was associated with a VE against symptomatic disease in infants of 44.4% (95% CI, 31.4%-54.9%). RCT data showed higher efficacy against hospitalization with the high-dose vaccine compared to the standard-dose vaccine among older adults.
争议与缺口
该综述强调了疫苗在不同年龄组和人群中的有效性存在异质性。尽管证据支持其降低重症和住院风险的效果,但针对老年人群的死亡率有效性显示出较宽的范围(29.0%至65.8%)。此外,综述指出,证据基础包括观察性研究和随机对照试验(RCT),这可能影响结论的强度。没有提及与目标实体相关的特定证据缺口,但综述承认需要进一步研究疫苗在某些人群中的有效性。
科研与实践启示
该综述表明,有必要继续进行 监测 和 疫苗 在高危人群(如老年人和婴儿)中的有效性研究。它还强调了监测 疫苗 安全性的重要性,特别是与其他 疫苗 如呼吸道合胞病毒或 COVID-19 免疫接种共同给药时。研究结果支持 季节性流感 疫苗 的持续使用,但强调需要改进 疫苗 配方以提高有效性,特别是在老年人中。

Influenza Vaccine Effectiveness and Safety for the 2026-2027 Respiratory Season.

Authors: Emily Senerth, Seyyedeh Mahsa Sheikholeslamian, Kapeena Sivakumaran, Michelle A. Watson, Rachel A. Lipson, Harseerat Saini, Jordan Falk, Neha Tangri, Ifeoluwa Babatunde, Melinda Wharton
Original abstract (English)
Importance: Influenza continues to cause significant mortality and morbidity in the US. Seasonal influenza vaccines reduce the severity of disease and prevent hospitalizations and death, especially among individuals at high risk. Objective: To identify and summarize the most recently published research spanning several influenza seasons reporting on the epidemiology of influenza in the US and the effectiveness and safety of US-licensed seasonal influenza vaccines, updating a prior evidence review through August 2025. Evidence Review: This systematic review searched Medline (PubMed), Embase, Cochrane CENTRAL, and Scopus from August 1, 2025, through June 30, 2026, for all English-language studies. Eligible publications reported on the epidemiology of influenza in the US or vaccine effectiveness and safety for any influenza vaccine formulation approved for use in the US. Data extraction included effectiveness and safety estimates reported among older adults; adults; during pregnancy; infants, children, and young adults; and persons who were immunocompromised. Risk of bias assessment was conducted. Findings: The search identified 69 eligible studies: 21 randomized clinical trials (RCTs) and 23 observational studies with a comparator group, 7 product safety studies without a comparator group, and 18 descriptive studies of disease burden. Across multiple seasons, influenza vaccines were associated with reduced risk of influenza-related hospitalization (vaccine effectiveness [VE] ranging from 19.0%; 95% CI, 0.0%-34.0% to 43.0%; 95% CI, 30.0%-53.0%) and mortality (VE ranging from 29.0%; 95% CI, 16.0%-40.0% to 65.8%; 95% CI, 54.6%-76.9%) among older adults. Vaccines were also associated with reduced risk of severe influenza among infants, children, adolescents, and young adults; a case-cohort analysis of national surveillance data over 8 seasons (2016-2020 and 2021-2025) among infants, children, and adolescents aged 6 months to 17 years reported that VE was 80.0% (95% CI, 75.0%-84.0%) against laboratory-confirmed influenza-associated deaths. Vaccination during pregnancy was associated with a VE against symptomatic disease in infants of 44.4% (95% CI, 31.4%-54.9%) based on a retrospective cohort study from 2011 to 2022. RCT data published in 2025 for seasons 2022 to 2025 reported higher efficacy against hospitalization with the high-dose compared with standard-dose vaccine among older adults (relative VE, 43.6%; 95% CI, 27.5%-56.3%). A large self-controlled case series among 9 973 703 individuals receiving vaccines found no association with increased risk of serious adverse events, including Guillain-Barré syndrome, after the seasonal influenza vaccine was administered alone or when coadministered with respiratory syncytial virus or COVID-19 immunizations. Another self-controlled case series reported no association with increased risk of febrile seizure during the interval 0 to 7 days after vaccination. Conclusions and Relevance: This systematic review found that evidence published August 1, 2025, through June 30, 2026, continued to demonstrate that influenza vaccines were associated with reduced risk of severe disease and hospitalization, especially among older adults, infants, children, and young adults. No new safety concerns were identified.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on seasonal influenza viruses, specifically human influenza A(H1N1)pdm09, A(H3N2), and B/Victoria lineage. The central question is to evaluate the effectiveness and safety of US-licensed seasonal influenza vaccines across multiple influenza seasons, with an emphasis on reducing severe disease and hospitalization risks among high-risk populations such as older adults, infants, children, and pregnant individuals.
Evidence base and method
This systematic review searched Medline (PubMed), Embase, Cochrane CENTRAL, and Scopus from August 1, 2025, through June 30, 2026, for all English-language studies. Eligible publications reported on the epidemiology of influenza in the US or vaccine effectiveness and safety for any influenza vaccine formulation approved for use in the US. Data extraction included effectiveness and safety estimates reported among various age groups and populations. A risk of bias assessment was conducted.
Consensus and conclusions
The review found that influenza vaccines were associated with reduced risk of severe disease and hospitalization, especially among older adults, infants, children, and young adults. The vaccine effectiveness (VE) against influenza-related hospitalization ranged from 19.0% (95% CI, 0.0%-34.0%) to 43.0% (95% CI, 30.0%-53.0%) among older adults. For infants, children, and adolescents, VE against laboratory-confirmed influenza-associated deaths was 80.0% (95% CI, 75.0%-84.0%) over 8 seasons. Vaccination during pregnancy was associated with a VE against symptomatic disease in infants of 44.4% (95% CI, 31.4%-54.9%). RCT data showed higher efficacy against hospitalization with the high-dose vaccine compared to the standard-dose vaccine among older adults.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights heterogeneity in vaccine effectiveness across different age groups and populations. While the evidence supports reduced risk of severe disease and hospitalization, the effectiveness against mortality among older adults showed a wide range (29.0% to 65.8%). Additionally, the review notes that the evidence base includes a mix of observational and RCT studies, which may affect the strength of conclusions. There is no mention of specific evidence gaps related to the target entities, but the review acknowledges the need for further research on vaccine effectiveness in certain populations.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that continued surveillance and research on vaccine effectiveness in high-risk populations, such as older adults and infants, are necessary. It also emphasizes the importance of monitoring vaccine safety, particularly when coadministered with other vaccines like respiratory syncytial virus or COVID-19 immunizations. The findings support the ongoing use of seasonal influenza vaccines but highlight the need for improved vaccine formulations to enhance effectiveness, especially in older adults.

新闻简报来自afludiary.blogspot.com的文章,发布于2026年9月4日,讨论了季节性流感和禽流感的活动情况,特别是2026年澳大利亚和新西兰H5N1的传播情况。文章强调了H5N1在过去四年中的传播趋势,指出其对哺乳动物的适应性增强以及传播范围的扩大。文本还提到了全球高致病性禽流感(HPAI)H5病毒的传播,包括其在各大洲的扩散以及候鸟在传播中的作用。文章特别指出,随着2026年南半球鸟类秋季迁徙已经开始,需要做好准备和应对措施。尽管文章未报告具体的确认或疑似病例,但强调了禽流感变化的特性以及新、更危险的基因型出现的潜在风险。该文章是一般性概述,未提供详细的病例数量或具体的风险评估。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Seasonal Flu Signals From New Zealand, Australia & Japan - afludiary.blogspot.com

News Brief
The article from afludiary.blogspot.com, published on September 4, 2026, discusses the seasonal flu and avian influenza activity, particularly focusing on the spread of H5N1 in Australia and New Zealand in 2026. It highlights the evolution of H5N1 over the past four years, noting its increased mammalian adaptation and broader host range. The text also references the global movement of HPAI H5 viruses, including their spread across continents and the role of migratory birds in transmission. The article emphasizes the need for preparedness and vaccination, especially as the 2026 fall southbound migration of birds has already begun. While no specific confirmed or suspected cases are reported, the text underscores the changing nature of avian flu and the potential for new, more dangerous genotypes to emerge. The article is a general overview and does not provide detailed case counts or specific risk assessments.
Original source text (English)
Avian Flu Diary Covering Pandemic and Seasonal Flu, Emerging Infectious Diseases, public health, community & Individual preparedness. NOTE: All AFD blogs are written by a human - any mistakes are solely mine. Monday, September 07, 2026 Pathogen's Progress H5 Affected States & Provinces June 2015 #19,326 Prior to 2021, North America had only had one brush with the Asian-origin HPAI H5 virus; a reassorted H5N8 subtype which appeared in South Korea in January of 2014 before beginning a world tour. It crossed the north Pacific - carried by migratory birds - the following fall, arriving in the Pacific Northwest in December 2014, sparking the first HPAI H5 epizootic in the United States and Canada (see map above). That H5Nx virus, however, disappeared completely the following summer, apparently still ill-suited for long-term carriage by wild and migratory birds (see PNAS: The Enigma Of Disappearing HPAI H5 In North American Migratory Waterfowl). That limitation would evaporate in the summer of 2016 when H5N8 reassorted into a far more robust threat (probably in China or Siberia) - arriving in Europe in October - and sparking their biggest (at that time) epizootic on record. EID Journal: Reassorted HPAI H5N8 Clade 2.3.4.4. - Germany 2016). This new H5N8 was not only deadlier to some bird species, it was more easily carried by others, increasing its range and impact dramatically over the next 6 months. We also saw this virus spin off several short-lived subtypes, including H5N5, H5N3, and H5N9. Over the winter of 2016-17, this H5N8 virus spread from Europe, into the Middle East, and then North Africa. By late spring, 2017, it had crossed the equator for the first time, and set up shop in South Africa, making it the most successfully disseminated HPAI H5 virus we'd seen. A European H5N6 briefly appeared in 2018, but H5N8 held on until a new, reassorted H5N1 virus appeared in Europe in 2020, and began to supplant H5N8. Unlike previous H5Nx incarnations, this new H5N1 showed an increased affinity for infecting mammals, and a much wider avian host range (see DEFRA: The Unprecedented `Order Shift' In Wild Bird H5N1 Positives In Europe & The UK). By 2022 this `new and improved' H5N1 virus had crossed both the Atlantic and Pacific oceans, spreading first to North America, followed by South America, the Antarctic, and most recently (2026) into Australia/New Zealand. While avian flu activity still decreased during the hotter months, no longer did HPAI H5 disappear completely each summer. It could be maintained (at low levels) in local birds, livestock, small mammals, and likely even the environment. All of which makes the HPAI H5 threat of today far different from what it was even a decade ago. The H5N1 virus of today has expanded not only its geographic range, but its host range as well. And it isn't done evolving. Since its arrival in 2021, the H5Nx virus has diversified into more than 100 distinct genotypes in North America alone, with scores of others emerging around the globe. A few notable North American examples include: In late 2023 a new B3.13 genotype emerged that could efficiently infect dairy cattle while mildly infecting dozens of humans Last fall we saw 3 new genotypes emerge (D1.1, D1.2, D1.3), with D1.1 producing more severe illness in humans. In October 2024 Canada experienced a multi-farm outbreak of emerging N1 genotype of H5N1 that was highly resistant to NAI antivirals (carrying the H275Y mutation) These variants illustrate just how quickly (and dramatically) the avian `fluscape' can change, and we generally see those changes in the fall, when migratory birds - which spent their summers in their high latitude roosting areas - return. A 2016 study (see Sci Repts.: Southward Autumn Migration Of Waterfowl Facilitates Transmission Of HPAI H5N1), suggests that waterfowl pick up new HPAI viruses in the spring (likely from poultry or terrestrial birds) on their northbound trip to their summer breeding spots - where they spread and potentially evolve - and then redistribute them on their southbound journey the following fall. Our own Arctic Refuge, where more than 200 bird species spend their summers, serves as a central hub, and funnels migratory birds south via all four North American Flyways. While this fall's HPAI viruses could be similar to what we saw last year - they might include new, potentially game-changing, genotypes - which could either increase or decrease their threat. Evolution is a double-edged sword, and what it adds it can also take away. But the trajectory of H5N1 over the past 4+ years has been towards more mammalian adaptation, and increased host range. We've also seen reports of H5N5 activity above the Arctic Circle (see Norwegian Veterinary Institute Reports Another HPAI H5N5 Infected Polar Bear on Svalbard Island), and H5N5 claimed the life of a Washington state bird keeper last fall. The APPA estimates `Eleven (11) million U.S. households own backyard chickens (a 28% increase from 2023)', yet a recent survey (see MMWR: Knowledge, Attitudes, and Practices Regarding Avian Influenza Among Owners of Backyard Flocks) found significant gaps in their knowledge of dealing with avian flu. While no one can predict with any confidence what HPAI will look like later this fall, the one thing we can be sure of is the 2026 fall southbound migration has already begun, and the time to prepare for its arrival grows shorter with every passing day. And if recent history is any guide, we should be preparing for additional surprises. Sunday, September 06, 2026 CDC Update: Flu Levels Remain Low, But COVID Increasing Across the U.S. Last Week This Week #19,325 Last week, after a summer of low COVID activity reported in the United States, we looked at Low But Increasing COVID reports across the nation. At that time, wastewater testing in 8 states indicated increased activity, while this week (see graphic above) the number of states has risen to 11. At the same time, the percentage of positive tests for COVID has jumped from 5.3 last week to 6.2 this week. Still low, but edging higher. The CDC's weekly summary reads: Testing, and reporting, of COVID cases remain low, and so the CDC's Nowcast is unable provide us with much in the way of details on this summer's variants. Last month the FDA approved a new JN.1 lineage XFG subvariant COVID vaccine (see CIDRAP FDA approves new COVID vaccines) which should be available later this fall. Currently, influenza and RSV are reportedly at very low levels (see chart below), but with fall approaching, those numbers are expected to rise in the months ahead. As reported a little over a week ago (see Japan MHLW Reports Unusually Early Start to the Fall Flu Season) a few countries in the Northern Hemisphere are already reporting substantial flu, even as the virus continues in the Southern Hemisphere. Admittedly, every flu season is different, and it is not unusual to see vastly different flu seasons in different parts of the world. So flu's early arrival in Japan may not be a harbinger for North America or Europe. This year's fall flu vaccines have been updated to reflect recent antigenic changes observed in all 3 seasonal flu types (see WHO Recommendations for Influenza Vaccine Composition for the 2026-2027 Northern Hemisphere Influenza Season). While no flu vaccine can promise 100% protection, it – along with practicing good flu hygiene (washing hands, wearing face masks in crowded indoor spaces, covering coughs, & staying home if sick) – remains your best strategy for avoiding the flu and staying healthy this winter. For some more reasons to consider getting the flu vaccine, you may wish to revisit: Eurosurveillance: Influenza Vaccination Attenuates Acute Myocardial Infarction and Stroke Risk Following Influenza Infection Nature: Influenza Vaccination & Major Cardiovascular Risk: a Systematic Review & Meta-Analysis The Lancet: Influenza Vaccine to Reduce Adverse Vascular Events in Patients with Heart Failure AHA: Study Shows Flu Shots Reduce Deaths From Heart Failure Saturday, September 05, 2026 #NPM26: Preparing For An El Niño Winter Credit NOAA Note: This is the 5th day of National Preparedness Month. Follow this year’s campaign on Twitter by searching for the #NatlPrep #BeReady or #PrepMonth hashtags. This month, as part of NPM26, I’ll be rerunning some updated preparedness essays, along with some new ones. #19,324 Two years ago I was forced to evacuate (for the 3rd time in 10 years) from my west-central Florida home due to the approach of hurricane Milton, which passed 50-60 miles to my south. While I live roughly 40 miles inland, and at a high (for Florida) elevation, we saw prolonged hurricane force winds, massive tree damage, and - thanks to 14" of rain in a few hours - a 500 year-flood. It took 4 days for the water to recede enough for me to return home. Luckily I had a `disaster buddy' who took me and my cat in for the duration. I was lucky, my home wasn't flooded, although some only a hundred yards away were. It took 4 days to get power back on, and a week to get internet (see Signs of Life). While my home took substantial wind damage, it was inland flooding that caused the worst damage to my community. Now, imagine that sort of rain dump happening - perhaps repeatedly - this winter in California. That's the growing concern for state officials (see ABC-7 News 'Super El Niño' strengthens, triggering preparations for possible flooding in LA County). Depending on the intensity, duration, and frequency of such storms, it could even lead to an ARkStorm event, which we first looked at 15 years ago in ARkStorm: California’s other "Big One". While it sounds like the plot from a bad made-for-cable Sci Fi film, this actually comes from the USGS Multi Hazards Demonstration Project (MHDP) analysis of a winter storm scenario with historical precedence. From the USGS website: Experts have designed a large, scientifically realistic meteorological event followed by an examination of the secondary hazards (for example, landslides and flooding), physical damages to the built environment, and social and economic consequences. The hypothetical storm depicted here would strike the U.S. West Coast and be similar to the intense California winter storms of 1861 and 1862 that left the central valley of California impassible. The storm is estimated to produce precipitation that in many places exceeds levels only experienced on average once every 500 to 1,000 years. USGS Video On ARkStorm We returned to this topic in 2022's California's ARkStorm Scenario Revisited, after the USGS released an updated assessment; one that suggested that as the climate grows hotter and dryer, it increases the odds of seeing another catastrophic ARkStorm event. Climate change is increasing the risk of a California megaflood XINGYING HUANG AND DANIEL L. SWAIN SCIENCE ADVANCES 12 Aug 2022 Vol 8, Issue 32 DOI: 10.1126/sciadv.abq0995 Abstract Despite the recent prevalence of severe drought, California faces a broadly underappreciated risk of severe floods. Here, we investigate the physical characteristics of “plausible worst case scenario” extreme storm sequences capable of giving rise to “megaflood” conditions using a combination of climate model data and high-resolution weather modeling. Using the data from the Community Earth System Model Large Ensemble, we find that climate change has already doubled the likelihood of an event capable of producing catastrophic flooding, but larger future increases are likely due to continued warming. We further find that runoff in the future extreme storm scenario is 200 to 400% greater than historical values in the Sierra Nevada because of increased precipitation rates and decreased snow fraction. These findings have direct implications for flood and emergency management, as well as broader implications for hazard mitigation and climate adaptation activities. (Continue . . . ) This is an extreme scenario - and remains a low probability event despite this winter's El Niño - but even something less severe could cause considerable damage, and potentially loss of life. So, the question is, how ready are you and your family for severe weather this winter? The LA County website advises: LA County is preparing its flood control and emergency response systems now. Crews are inspecting and maintaining dams, debris basins, channels, storm drains, and pump stations; monitoring burn areas, hillsides, and other areas at risk of flooding and debris flows; and preparing for evacuations, emergency shelter, and other storm response. County teams are also preparing to assist people experiencing homelessness, older adults, and others who may need additional help during severe weather. Here’s what residents can do now: Sign up for emergency alerts: Go to alert.lacounty.gov to receive emergency notifications, including evacuation warnings and orders. Know the risks where you live: If you are near a hillside, canyon, recent burn area, or area that can flood, learn your flood and mudflow risk before a storm. Make an evacuation plan: Know where you will go and how you will get there if you need to leave quickly. Have supplies ready: Keep enough food, water, medications, and other essentials for up to 10 days. Prepare your home: Clear gutters, downspouts, and storm drains, fix roof leaks, and get sandbags before heavy rain is in the forecast. Consider flood insurance: Standard homeowners insurance does not cover flood damage. Flood insurance is separate and can take at least 30 days to go into effect. Need help with mud or debris flow risk? Call LA County Public Works at 1-800-933-0930 to speak with an engineer. Know someone experiencing homelessness who needs help? Visit homeless.lacounty.gov/get-help/ But it isn't just California that could be impacted by a strong El Niño. Here in Florida - where we are enjoying the quietest Atlantic Hurricane Season in recent memory - we've a history of seeing strong winter storms during El Niño years, including the tornado outbreak of 1998 which killed 42 people. Predicting exactly where unusual or severe weather will occur this winter due to El Niño is impossible, but generally, the jet stream across the United States shifts storm tracks to the southern tier of states. While this doesn't guarantee that other areas will get a free ride this winter, it does suggest that California may be unusually wet, and ice storms and blizzards may be more likely to develop across the deep south this coming winter. Exactly where that may happen is unknowable, but five years ago (Feb 2021) 3 successive winter storms swept across Texas, Louisiana, and Oklahoma, causing the worst collapse of the Texas energy grid on record (see Texas: The Latest - But Not The Last - Grid Down Crisis). At least 4.5 million homes were without power during a week of bitter winter temperatures, resulting in hundreds of deaths and tens of billions of dollars of damage (see City of Austin & Travis County 2021 WINTER STORM URI AFTER-ACTION REVIEW). While I can't tell you where the next natural disaster will strike, or how bad it will be, being prepared for whatever comes is your best defense. So, if the grid goes down, or flood waters rise, or the ground begins to shake . . . do you already have? A battery operated NWS Emergency Radio to find out what was going on, and to get vital instructions from emergency officials A decent first-aid kit, so that you can treat injuries Enough non-perishable food and water on hand to feed and hydrate your family (including pets) for the duration A way to provide light when the grid is down. A way to cook safely without electricity A way to purify or filter water A way to handle basic sanitation and waste disposal. A way to stay cool (fans) or warm when the power is out. A small supply of cash to use in case credit/debit machines are not working An emergency plan, including meeting places, emergency out-of-state contact numbers, a disaster buddy, and in case you must evacuate, a bug-out bag Spare supply of essential prescription medicines that you or your family may need A way to entertain yourself, or your kids, during a prolonged blackout If not, you've got some important work to do. A good place to get started is by visiting Ready.gov. Friday, September 04, 2026 #NatlPrep: 75% of Americans At Risk Of Experiencing A Damaging Earthquake Note: This is the 4th day of National Preparedness Month. Follow this year’s campaign on Twitter by searching for the #NatlPrep #BeReady or #PrepMonth hashtags. This month, as part of NPM26, I’ll be rerunning some updated preparedness essays, along with some new ones. #19,323 Over the past 3 months, in addition to hundreds of mild-to-moderate earthquakes, the world has seen 3 devastating seismic events: In early June, the Philippines was struck by a 7.8 quake offshore of Mindanao, killing more than 100 people, and damaging more than 100 thousand homes. Two weeks later Venezuela was struck by twin quakes (7.2 and 7.5) - 39 seconds apart - killing more than 6500 people, and destroying tens of thousands of buildings. Three weeks after that (August 10th) Colombia was hit by a 7.4 quake, killing > 330 people, and causing massive infrastructure collapse. While we like to think that these sorts of events can't happen here in the United States, Canada, or Europe, the truth is more sobering. In 2006 the USGS calculated that earthquakes posed a significant risk to 75 million Americans living in 39 States. A 2015 study, published in the journal Earthquake Spectra, nearly doubled – to 143 million - the number of Americans who live or work in areas susceptible to potentially damaging ground shaking (see USGS: Nearly Half Of U.S. Population Exposed to Potentially Damaging Earthquakes). In January of 2024 the USGS unveiled a new seismic risk map (see top of blog), which increased the percentage of the population at risk of experiencing a damaging earthquake to nearly 75% (roughly 240 million peopl...

FDA 批准 Moderna 的 mRNA 季节性流感 疫苗 - ABC News - 突发新闻、最新新闻和视频

FDA approves Moderna's mRNA seasonal flu vaccine - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos
新闻简报美国食品药品监督管理局(FDA)于2026年8月6日批准了Moderna的mRNA季节性流感疫苗疫苗、mFLUSIVA,用于50岁以上的成年人。这一批准是基于临床试验结果显示,疫苗相比当前标准疫苗疫苗可使确诊流感病例减少27%。其安全性特征相似,但更多人报告了轻微的不良反应。该疫苗预计将在2026-2027年呼吸道季节中提供,并可能帮助降低老年人的严重疾病、住院和死亡风险。Moderna计划继续监测该疫苗在65岁及以上人群中的表现,并向FDA提交相关数据。FDA最初表示不会审查该申请,但在Moderna承诺进行额外研究后改变了决定。这一批准反映了mRNA技术在通过快速生产及适应流行毒株应对公共卫生挑战方面的潜力。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

FDA approves Moderna's mRNA seasonal flu vaccine - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos

News Brief
The Food and Drug Administration (FDA) approved Moderna's mRNA seasonal flu vaccine, mFLUSIVA, on August 6, 2026, for use in adults over 50. This approval comes after clinical trials showed the vaccine led to 27% fewer confirmed flu cases compared to the current standard vaccine. The safety profile was similar, but more people reported mild side effects. The vaccine is expected to be available for the 2026-2027 respiratory season and may help reduce severe illness, hospitalization, and death in older adults. Moderna plans to monitor its performance against the high-dose vaccine for those 65 and older and submit data to the FDA. The FDA initially stated it would not review the application but reversed its decision after Moderna committed to additional studies. This approval reflects the potential of mRNA technology to address public health challenges through rapid production and adaptability to circulating strains.

新冠(COVID-19)传播模式与季节性流感趋同,暴发模式转变 - 病毒学家 - 泰国PBS世界频道

COVID-19 aligns with seasonal flu as outbreak pattern shifts - virologist - Thai PBS World
新闻简报该文章报道称,泰国的 COVID-19 病例呈现模式 暴发 已转变为更接近 季节性流感 的模式,据朱拉隆功大学医学院临床病毒学卓越中心负责人 Yong 博士指出。他提到,该病毒的传播时间比往常更晚,近期病例数达到高峰,预计随着学校假期的开始,10 月将出现下降。该分析将当前的 COVID-19 传播情况与历史模式进行比较,指出通常病毒会在宋干节和早雨季后出现激增,但今年的模式出现了明显偏离。文章还提到,流感病例数是 COVID-19 病例数的三倍,达到约 350,000 例,而 COVID-19 病例数约为 100,000 例,但两者 死亡率 率相似。这表明当前主导的 COVID-19 子株可能并不显著比季节性流感更严重。这些发现基于报告的数据和观察,未提及任何特定的调查或防控措施。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026-09-05。文章描述暴发模式在过去两个月发生了转变,预计感染高峰将在10月逐渐消退。
地点与对象
分析涉及全国范围内的病毒传播,提到了朱拉隆功大学医学院和一般人群。
事件
永博士指出,泰国新冠(COVID-19)的暴发传播模式已转变为更接近季节性流感的模式,病例数较以往推迟达到高峰,预计10月会下降。这是基于过去六年对病毒传播和行为的观察得出的结论。
规模、影响与风险
The article reports that flu cases were three times higher than COVID-19 cases, reaching about 350,000 compared to approximately 100,000 for COVID-19. However, mortality rates for both respiratory illnesses are similar, with 16 deaths among COVID-19 patients and about 50 among those with influenza. The current dominant sub-strain of COVID-19 is inferred to be no longer significantly more severe than seasonal flu.
应对、状态与不确定性

B routine priorityPublic-health news

COVID-19 aligns with seasonal flu as outbreak pattern shifts - virologist - Thai PBS World

News Brief
The article reports that the outbreak pattern of COVID-19 in Thailand has shifted to align more closely with that of seasonal influenza, according to Dr. Yong, head of the Centre of Excellence in Clinical Virology at Chulalongkorn University's Faculty of Medicine. He noted that the virus has been spreading later than usual, with case numbers peaking in recent months and expected to decline in October as school holidays begin. The analysis compares the current spread of COVID-19 with historical patterns, highlighting that the virus typically surged after the Songkran festival and the early rainy season, but this year's pattern has diverged sharply. The article also mentions that flu cases were three times higher than COVID-19 cases, reaching about 350,000 compared to approximately 100,000, but mortality rates for both are similar. This suggests that the current dominant sub-strain of COVID-19 may not be significantly more severe than seasonal flu. The findings are based on reported data and observations, with no specific investigation or containment measures mentioned.

Accessibility Tools Increase Text Decrease Text Grayscale Link Underline Readable Font Reset NEWS NEWS POLITICS POLITICS BUSINESS & ECONOMY BUSINESS & ECONOMY GENERAL GENERAL AROUND THAILAND AROUND THAILAND ASEAN ASEAN WORLD WORLD IN-DEPTH IN-DEPTH ANALYSIS ANALYSIS OPINION OPINION PROGRAMMES PROGRAMMES MORE MORE GALLERY GALLERY QUOTES QUOTES PODCAST PODCAST ABOUT US ABOUT US SEARCH SEARCH NEWS POLITICS BUSINESS & ECONOMY GENERAL AROUND THAILAND ASEAN WORLD IN-DEPTH ANALYSIS OPINION PROGRAMMES MORE GALLERY QUOTES PODCAST ABOUT US SEARCH COVID-19 aligns with seasonal flu as outbreak pattern shifts - virologist Shared Share COVID-19 aligns with seasonal flu as outbreak pattern shifts - virologist Health 05 Sep 2026 by Thai PBS World LISTEN Thailand's pattern and speed of COVID-19 spread this year differs significantly from previous years, with case numbers peaking later than usual, but expected to decline in October, according to prominent virologist Dr. Yong Poovorawan. Writing in a post on his Facebook page today, Dr. Yong, head of the Centre of Excellence in Clinical Virology at Chulalongkorn University's Faculty of Medicine, noted that COVID-19 activity remained extremely low during the early months of the year, before resurging over the past two months. Infections are currently at their peak and are projected to subside in October as school holidays begin. Over the past six years, the virus typically spread rapidly following the Songkran festival, continued through the early rainy season and began tapering off in August. The 2026 pattern has diverged sharply from that seen over the previous two years. Dr. Yong highlighted that COVID-19 is increasingly behaving like seasonal influenza. Influenza infections surged early in the year, during the cool season, and experienced a slight delay before peaking alongside COVID-19 over the past two months. Although flu cases were three times higher, reaching roughly 350,000 compared to about COVID’s 100,000, mortality rates for both respiratory illnesses are similar. Official data reports 16 deaths among COVID-19 patients and approximately 50 among those with influenza, from which one might infer that the current dominant sub-strain of COVID-19 is no longer significantly more severe than seasonal flu. 食品城药房提供季节性流感 疫苗 - knoxtntoday.com

Food City Pharmacy offers seasonal flu vaccine - knoxtntoday.com
新闻简报Food City Pharmacy in Knox County, Tennessee, is offering the seasonal flu vaccine during the immunization season, as reported on September 4, 2026. The service is available at multiple locations and is intended for individuals 12 years and older, including high-risk groups such as those 65 or older, residents of long-term care facilities, individuals with comorbid conditions, and pregnant women. The CDC recommends vaccination for these groups to reduce the risk of severe illness, hospitalization, and death. Vaccinations are available on a walk-up basis and will continue throughout flu season, depending on vaccine availability. The pharmacy also offers other preventative health care options, including the COVID-19 vaccine, shingles, pneumococcal, and RSV vaccines. Insurance, including Medicare Part B, is accepted with many having zero copays. The seasonal flu vaccine protects against the most common strains of influenza, which are typically most prevalent during the fall and winter months, with activity increasing in October and November and peaking between December and February. The service is part of the pharmacy’s commitment to community health, following CDC guidelines.

Food City Pharmacy offers seasonal flu vaccine - knoxtntoday.com

News Brief
Food City Pharmacy in Knox County, Tennessee, is offering the seasonal flu vaccine during the immunization season, as reported on September 4, 2026. The service is available at multiple locations and is intended for individuals 12 years and older, including high-risk groups such as those 65 or older, residents of long-term care facilities, individuals with comorbid conditions, and pregnant women. The CDC recommends vaccination for these groups to reduce the risk of severe illness, hospitalization, and death. Vaccinations are available on a walk-up basis and will continue throughout flu season, depending on vaccine availability. The pharmacy also offers other preventative health care options, including the COVID-19 vaccine, shingles, pneumococcal, and RSV vaccines. Insurance, including Medicare Part B, is accepted with many having zero copays. The seasonal flu vaccine protects against the most common strains of influenza, which are typically most prevalent during the fall and winter months, with activity increasing in October and November and peaking between December and February. The service is part of the pharmacy’s commitment to community health, following CDC guidelines.

It honors the people who keep a community moving About The Author Betsi James 2 Comments Lisa Harper on September 4, 2026 at 12:31 pm We are often told that this is “flu season”. When I did a quick search online, I found that in the US flu season starts about October and goes through May. Peak flu activity is December to March. We are also often told that the “best” way to prevent flu is with the flu vaccine. I’d like to challenge what you have been told. I don’t think “Flu Season” is the correct term. I think the flu is simply the effect and not the cause. Sure, we don’t get enough sunshine and therefore vitamin D since it is colder weather and we stay inside more and that could decrease our immune systems. I still don’t think that is the cause... I’ve even heard people say that it’s because we are in closer contact with other people so it spreads more and faster.... Nope, still don’t think that is the main cause. Here is what I think is the cause and what we should be calling this time of year instead of “flu season”: it is Sugar Season! Follow me here while I give you a little story time-line... October: Halloween is at the end of the month. We see the candy hit the shelves in vast quantities before the end of September. People often stock up on the candy they “plan to give out to the kids on Halloween night”. Many actually have to repurchase before that night since all the candy has already been consumed! (they may also not actually have any trick-or-treaters at all!) November: Thanksgiving. Lots of food! Lots of dessert!! Need I say more?? December: Christmas! Parties....often several of them. Family get-togethers, again, often several. Plenty of food and desserts to go around here, too! January: Happy New Year! Let’s toast with some champagne and snacks, and treats, and some more alcohol! (Starting to get the picture?) February: Valentine’s Day! Chocolate!!! Those strange candy hearts with phrases on them! March/April (depending on the year): Easter! Bunny’s laying chocolate eggs...lots of baskets filled with sweet treats and hunts with treats as well. May: Time to get outside and start having picnics in the sunshine. More about the grill and less about the sweets. Get the picture? From October to March/April we are loaded with holidays that seem to focus on sugary desserts. Starting in about May through September we are more focused on being outside and grilling rather than quite as many sweets. I’m not saying there are none, but there seems to be less. Add in additional sunshine and movement and our immune systems are simply more likely to be stronger during the summer. Here is what sugar does to your immune system: it decreases your immune system’s ability to detect and fight invaders by 50%!! And it does so for about 5 hours! See why I think it should be called “sugar season” instead of “flu season”? Knowing this about sugar and your immune system, do we really need a flu shot? (There are risks with any medical treatment that goes in your body and the flu shot is no exception!) Instead, how about we be mindful of what we choose to consume this time of year. I will cover more on this next month as well. For now, let that Halloween candy stay on the shelves. If you want a sweet treat, try to pick a healthier option. This can sometimes just mean you make it at home with healthier ingredients that buying already prepared. Stick to eating the things you REALLY like. Don’t just put anything in there! The more mindful you are of how much sugar you are consuming the more likely your immune system will be strong enough to fight off even the most stubborn viruses. Also, a lack of vitamin D, as well as other essential minerals and nutrients that get zapped from the body. This happens when the body is depleted due to the drug mugging effect of prescription meds. Poor sleep and stress get thrown into the mix as well. Take care of your body and it will take care of you. Our body is intelligent if we allow it to be. Innovation A New mRNA Flu Vaccine Is Here, But Scientists Still Cannot Predict How The 2026 Season Will Unfold Published Sep 03, 2026 2:00 PM EDT By Lucia Carter Karen Ducey | Getty Images Share on Twitter Share on Facebook Share on Pocket Flu vaccines are updated every year, but the 2026–27 season brings a notable change: an mRNA vaccine has joined the options available to some adults in the United States. The vaccine, called MFLUSIVA, was developed by Moderna and is approved for adults aged 50 and older. Unlike traditional flu vaccines that use established approaches to present influenza proteins to the immune system, it uses messenger RNA, the same basic technology that made headlines during the COVID-19 pandemic. Its arrival comes as scientists prepare for another unpredictable flu season. Influenza viruses constantly change, which means researchers have to decide months in advance which strains vaccines should target. For 2026–27, all three components of U.S. seasonal flu vaccines were updated, including a change aimed at the influenza A(H3N2) subclade K virus that circulated widely during the previous season. So the question heading into the new season is not simply whether there is a new vaccine. It is whether this new technology can offer a meaningful advantage against a virus that keeps changing. How the New mRNA Vaccine Works Messenger RNA, or mRNA, carries temporary instructions that cells can use to make proteins. An mRNA vaccine uses those instructions to prompt the body to produce a harmless piece of a virus. The immune system recognizes it as foreign and builds a response, preparing the body to respond more quickly if it encounters the actual virus. That approach is different from many conventional flu vaccines, but the goal is familiar: train the immune system before influenza arrives. MFLUSIVA was evaluated in a Phase 3 study involving more than 20,000 adults aged 50 and older. In the FDA's review, the vaccine showed a 26.6% relative vaccine efficacy compared with a standard-dose flu vaccine against laboratory-confirmed influenza illness caused by influenza A or B. The result is encouraging, but it does not mean the mRNA vaccine is automatically the best choice for everyone. For adults 65 and older, the evidence is more nuanced. The vaccine produced immune responses that supported its approval, but the FDA requires additional research to confirm clinical benefit in this older population. That distinction is important because an immune response measured in a laboratory is not the same thing as proving that fewer people will actually get sick. Why Flu Vaccines Change Every Year Even a highly effective vaccine has to contend with influenza's ability to evolve. The viruses circulating in one flu season can differ from those that dominated the previous year. Scientists therefore monitor influenza around the world and select vaccine strains that are expected to be most relevant when the next season begins. The 2026–27 U.S. vaccines reflect that process. All three vaccine components were updated from the previous season, with particular attention to the H3N2 subclade K virus. This is also why early flu activity in the Southern Hemisphere attracts attention in the months before winter arrives in North America. It can provide clues about which influenza viruses are circulating and how they are behaving, although it cannot reliably predict exactly how the Northern Hemisphere season will unfold. The uncertainty is part of what makes influenza different from illnesses caused by a single, relatively stable virus. When Should People Get The Flu Vaccine? The arrival of a new vaccine does not change the basic timing of seasonal vaccination. CDC guidance says annual influenza vaccination is recommended for people aged 6 months and older who do not have a contraindication. For most people who need one dose, vaccination is ideally offered in September or October. The timing is deliberate. Immunity can weaken over the course of the flu season, so getting vaccinated too early may not always be preferable, particularly for some older adults. Still, waiting indefinitely is not useful either. CDC guidance says vaccination should continue after October while influenza viruses are circulating and vaccines remain available. For people aged 65 and older, the CDC preferentially recommends certain higher-dose or adjuvanted vaccines when available. MFLUSIVA's approval for adults aged 50 and older gives some people another option, but there is no blanket recommendation that the mRNA vaccine should replace other appropriate vaccines. What This Could Mean For Future Flu Vaccines The significance of an mRNA flu vaccine extends beyond this year's vaccination appointments. Influenza has long presented a manufacturing challenge because vaccine strains have to be selected and produced ahead of the season. mRNA technology has attracted interest partly because its platform can be adapted to different targets. But whether that translates into better protection in the real world still has to be demonstrated. Flu vaccine effectiveness varies from season to season depending on the viruses circulating, how closely they match the vaccine, and characteristics of the people being vaccinated. CDC conducts effectiveness studies every season for precisely that reason. For now, the new mRNA option is best viewed as another development in the long effort to keep seasonal influenza under control, rather than a breakthrough that settles the problem. As the 2026–27 season approaches, surveillance will reveal whether the strains scientists anticipated are the ones that actually dominate. The answer may ultimately tell us as much about the future of flu vaccination as the arrival of the new technology itself. 流感 - 疫苗,预防,暴发 - 布拉提卡

Influenza - Vaccines, Prevention, Outbreaks - Britannica
新闻简报该文章来自布拉提卡,讨论了季节性流感病毒及其全球影响,重点聚焦于大流行病的准备。文章概述了世界卫生组织(WHO)的六阶段大流行病警报系统,包括对潜在大流行病早期迹象的监测,例如孤立的动物到人类的传播。文本解释了随着病毒传播并在多个国家引发持续的人类到人类的传播,警报级别会提高,从而导致更紧迫的缓解措施。文章描述了流感是一种影响所有年龄段的疾病,尽管在儿童和年轻人中更为常见。文章还指出,流感暴发在较冷的月份更为频繁,可能导致严重的并发症,如肺炎或支气管炎,这些并发症可能致命,尤其是在老年人中。此外,文章还提到了抗病毒药物抗病毒药物,如奥司他韦和扎那米韦,这些药物对甲型和乙型流感病毒均有效,并指出病毒抗药性已降低了某些治疗的有效性。文章未报告特定的暴发或确诊病例,但提供了关于流感传播、症状和预防策略的一般信息。
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章讨论了大流行流感的准备,并提到WHO在全球范围内监测流感疾病活动,最近更新时间为2026年9月2日。它还提到了流感的一般季节性特征,即在较冷的月份更为频繁。
地点与对象
内容是一般性的,未指定特定地点或人群。它将流感描述为一种影响所有年龄段的全球健康问题,其中儿童和年轻人的发病率较高。
事件
文章概述了WHO的流感大流行病准备计划,包括大流行病警报的六个阶段。它描述了从对动物到人类的传播早期监测到最终阶段的广泛人类到人类的传播和持续疾病的发展过程。
规模、影响与风险
文章指出,流感大流行病可能迅速摧毁大片地区,并可能以波浪形式出现,持续数月。它还提到,如肺炎或支气管炎等并发症可能导致死亡,尤其是在有基础疾病的老年个体中。
应对、状态与不确定性
WHO的大流行病准备计划包括逐步升级的紧急阶段,早期阶段侧重于控制暴发,而后期阶段则侧重于缓解策略。文章未提供关于当前调查或确诊病例的具体细节。

Influenza - Vaccines, Prevention, Outbreaks - Britannica

News Brief
The article from Britannica discusses seasonal influenza viruses and their global impact, focusing on pandemic preparedness. It outlines the World Health Organization's (WHO) six-phase pandemic alert system, which includes monitoring for early signs of potential pandemics, such as isolated animal-to-human transmission. The text explains that as the virus spreads and causes sustained human-to-human transmission in multiple countries, the alert level increases, leading to more urgent mitigation efforts. Influenza is described as a disease that affects all ages, though it is more common among children and young adults. The article highlights that influenza outbreaks are more frequent during colder months and can lead to severe complications, such as pneumonia or bronchitis, which may result in death, especially among older individuals. It also mentions the use of antiviral drugs like oseltamivir and zanamivir, which are effective against both influenza A and B viruses, and notes that viral resistance has reduced the effectiveness of some treatments. The content does not report on specific outbreaks or confirmed cases but provides general information on influenza transmission, symptoms, and prevention strategies.
